=== NOTES 6:0 | Sep–4 Nov 2026: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | P4 Zero Poverty, NTR Bharosa Pensions and TTD Insurance
[0:0:0] # P4 – Public-Private-People Partnership (APPSC FSO PYQ)
[0:0:1] - **P4 = Public-Private-People Partnership**; launched by CM Naidu and Dy CM Pawan Kalyan at **Velagapudi, Amaravati** on **Ugadi, 30 March 2025** (a Sunday) – not Independence Day, Republic Day or Diwali.
[0:0:2] - Rolled out statewide from **19 August 2025**.
[0:0:3] - **Model:** the **top 10%** of society adopt the **bottom 20%**; mentors/donors = **Margadarsis**, beneficiary families = **Bangaru Kutumbams** (golden families); voluntary help (aid, mentoring), not a cash-transfer scheme.
[0:0:4] - About **19.16 lakh** Bangaru families identified.
[0:0:5] - **First anniversary (30 Mar 2026, Tirupati):** **1.06 lakh Margadarsis** had adopted 10.3 lakh families; goal restated as a **poverty-free society by 2047** (earlier: zero poverty by 2029).
[0:0:6] # NTR Bharosa and NSAP pensions
[0:0:7] - **NTR Bharosa** = AP's social security pensions; about **₹76,000 crore** disbursed by October 2026.
[0:0:8] - **October 2026:** **₹2,692.34 crore** released; door-to-door distribution from 1 October 2026.
[0:0:9] - From **1 October 2026** the **Central share** of **NSAP** (**National Social Assistance Programme**) pensions goes straight to **Aadhaar-linked bank accounts** via **SNA-SPARSH** – **9.34 lakh** beneficiaries, ₹382.82 crore; the **State share is still paid in cash**.
[0:0:10] # TTD pilgrim insurance
[0:0:11] TABLE head: Cover | Amount
  [0:0:11.0] Natural death of pilgrims in Tirumala (first-of-its-kind) | ₹2 lakh
  [0:0:11.1] Accidental insurance | Raised from ₹3 lakh to ₹10 lakh
[0:0:12] ~~ Sources: Deccan Chronicle 31 Mar 2025 "Naidu, Pawan launch P4 initiative" (deccanchronicle.com, fetched); CARE Feb 2026 p.14 (P4 – Margadarsis); app notes 6:0 (sub 1)
## SUB 1 | Amarajeevi Jaladhara – Jal Jeevan Mission 2.0 (5 Oct 2026)
[0:1:0] # Launch facts
[0:1:1] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [0:1:1.0] Name | Amarajeevi Jaladhara – AP's State programme under Jal Jeevan Mission 2.0
  [0:1:1.1] Launched by | CM N. Chandrababu Naidu, with Dy CM Pawan Kalyan
  [0:1:1.2] Place and date | Proddatur, YSR Kadapa district, 5 October 2026 (a Monday; World Habitat Day 2026)
  [0:1:1.3] Investment | ₹30,156 crore (₹30,156.12 crore); 50,315 drinking-water works
  [0:1:1.4] Coverage | 90 lakh households, about 48,000 rural habitations, about 3.3 crore people
  [0:1:1.5] Water norm | 55 litres per person per day
  [0:1:1.6] Other targets | 27.36 lakh more rural families; 13,075 overhead tanks; 61.60 lakh tap connections
[0:1:2] - **Focus:** household tap connections in **rural** habitations – not urban households, and not launched at Amaravati.
[0:1:3] - **Jal Jeevan Mission:** launched **15 August 2019**, **Ministry of Jal Shakti**; JJM 2.0 (Cabinet, 10 March 2026) turns it into a service-based model, target all rural households by December 2028.
[0:1:4] # Other announcements at Proddatur
[0:1:5] TABLE head: Item | Detail
  [0:1:5.0] Irrigation | 36 projects, ₹35,000 crore, over 3 years
  [0:1:5.1] Polavaram | To be completed before the next Godavari Pushkaram
  [0:1:5.2] River interlinking | Godavari–Krishna–Cauvery
  [0:1:5.3] Palle Panduga | Rural infrastructure works worth ₹11,085 crore
  [0:1:5.4] Housing | 19 lakh houses by 2029
  [0:1:5.5] Rayalaseema Horticulture Hub | ₹1 lakh crore
[0:1:6] > ₹30,156 crore = JJM 2.0; ₹11,085 crore = Palle Panduga; ₹35,000 crore = 36 irrigation projects; ₹1 lakh crore = Rayalaseema Horticulture Hub.
[0:1:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:0 (sub 0, Carpediem CA Oct 2026); Rocket CA 2026 Part 01 p.155 (JJM 2019, Ministry of Jal Shakti; JJM 2.0)
## SUB 2 | Housing, Swachh Andhra and World Habitat Day
[0:2:0] # India's first 3D concrete-printed government houses
[0:2:1] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [0:2:1.0] First | India's first 3D Concrete Printing (3DCP) government housing pilot
  [0:2:1.1] Place | Burripalem, Tenali constituency (Guntur)
  [0:2:1.2] Houses | 100 houses, 325 sq ft each
  [0:2:1.3] Scheme | PMAY-G 2.0 (rural)
  [0:2:1.4] Builder | CBRI (Central Building Research Institute), Roorkee (CSIR) – not IIT Madras
  [0:2:1.5] Process | 3D CAD design → concrete mix → robotic/gantry printer → layer-by-layer printing (no manual brick layout)
[0:2:2] # PMAY-U 2.0 award and World Habitat Day
[0:2:3] - **AP ranked 1st** among Best Performing States for **urban housing** under **PMAY-U 2.0 (2025–26)**; award on World Habitat Day, New Delhi.
[0:2:4] - **World Habitat Day:** **first Monday of October**, set by the **UNGA**, first observed **1986**; in 2026 on **5 October**.
[0:2:5] - **Calendar:** October 2026 begins on a **Thursday** → **Thursday, Friday, Saturday** occur five times; first Monday 5 Oct → third Monday **19 October**.
[0:2:6] # Swachh Andhra
[0:2:7] - **Swachh Andhra Awards 2026:** presented by CM Naidu at Vijayawada on **Gandhi Jayanti (Swachh Diwas), 2 October 2026**.
[0:2:8] TABLE head: Winner | Award
  [0:2:8.0] Vijayawada Municipal Corporation | State-level Swachh City
  [0:2:8.1] Guntur Municipal Corporation | Swachh Municipality (3 lakh+ population)
  [0:2:8.2] Mangalagiri-Tadepalli MC | Swachh Municipality (1–3 lakh)
[0:2:9] - **"Swarna Andhra – Swachh Andhra" day:** the **third Saturday of every month**; implemented by the **Swachha Andhra Corporation**.
[0:2:10] - **CLAP = Clean Andhra Pradesh** (Jagananna Swachha Sankalpam), launched **2 October 2021** under **Swachh Bharat Mission 2.0**; 4,097 garbage vehicles.
[0:2:11] ~~ Sources: app notes 6:0 (sub 2, Carpediem CA Oct 2026); calendar checked (Python datetime)
## SUB 3 | Capital, Districts, Secretariats, BC Quota and Governance
[0:3:0] # Capital and districts
[0:3:1] - **Amaravati** is the capital under the **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2026** – **Act 7 of 2026**, President's assent **6 April 2026**; amends **Section 5(2)** of the 2014 Act, with effect from **2 June 2024**.
[0:3:2] - AP has **28 districts** since **31 December 2025**: new **Polavaram** and **Markapuram** districts, with **five** new revenue divisions.
[0:3:3] # Village/ward secretariats renamed (GSWS Act amended 2026)
[0:3:4] TABLE head: Old name | New name
  [0:3:4.0] Grama Sachivalayam | Swarna Gramam
  [0:3:4.1] Ward Sachivalayam | Swarna Ward (Swarna Wardu)
[0:3:5] - About **15,425** secretariats; linked to Swarna Andhra Vision 2047; the old **volunteer system was NOT revived**.
[0:3:6] # BC reservation in local bodies (Oct 2026)
[0:3:7] - **1 Oct 2026:** AP **High Court** struck down G.O.s giving **34% BC reservation** in rural local bodies – total reservation must not exceed **50%**.
[0:3:8] - **9 Oct 2026:** **Supreme Court dismissed** the State's appeal – the quota stays struck down (it was NOT upheld).
[0:3:9] # Uniform college affiliation committee (27 Oct 2026)
[0:3:10] - Head: **APSCHE Chairman**; convener: Commissioner of Higher Education; VCs of **Andhra University**, Adikavi Nannaya University, JNTU Kakinada; registrars of SVU, Krishna University, JNTU Anantapur.
[0:3:11] - Studies Maharashtra, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Kerala, UP; report within **one month** (not three).
[0:3:12] - **UGC** works under the **UGC Act, 1956**.
[0:3:13] # AP One app
[0:3:14] - Single app of the **Real Time Governance Society (RTGS)** for about **150 government services** (Telugu and English).
[0:3:15] ~~ Sources: Praveen daily CA 27 Oct 2026 p.7 (college affiliation committee); CARE Apr 2026 p.28 (Polavaram district); app notes 6:0 (sub 8)
## SUB 4 | Sanjeevani – Tribal Development Strategy (Araku)
[0:4:0] - Launched by CM **N. Chandrababu Naidu** at **Ganneli, Araku Valley**, **Alluri Sitarama Raju** district.
[0:4:1] - **Aim:** **quality healthcare for remote tribal families** and **eliminating tribal poverty**; five prongs – infrastructure, healthcare, education, employment, welfare.
[0:4:2] TABLE head: Area | Initiative
  [0:4:2.0] Roads | Adivathalli Bata – 900 km, ₹1,000 crore
  [0:4:2.1] Emergency | 150 vehicles in ASR district
  [0:4:2.2] Health | Tribal Health Infrastructure Mission – ₹250 crore
  [0:4:2.3] Mobile health | 104 ambulances with 42 diagnostic tests
  [0:4:2.4] Mothers | Talli Bidda Express – transport for pregnant women
  [0:4:2.5] Education | Agreement with IIT Madras – IIT and NEET coaching
  [0:4:2.6] Coffee | Expand by 1 lakh acres (coffee and pepper)
[0:4:3] - **Welfare convergence:** Talliki Vandanam, Annadata Sukhibhava, Stree Shakti, Auto Driver Service – **Amma Vodi is NOT in the list** (it is the old name of Talliki Vandanam).
[0:4:4] - **G.O. 3** (tribal-area jobs for locals) was **annulled by the Supreme Court**.
[0:4:5] ~~ Sources: CARE Sep 2026 p.3 (Ganneli, Adivathalli Bata); app notes 6:0 (sub 4)
## SUB 5 | Health and Food Safety Decisions
[0:5:0] # AP Human Organ Transplantation Amendment Act 2026
[0:5:1] - Approved by the Governor as **AP Act No. 25 of 2026**; covers tissues; **grandparents and grandchildren** added as near relatives; **swap transplantation** recognised.
[0:5:2] - Parent law **THOTA** enacted **1994** (amended 2011); **Jeevandan** = AP's **deceased organ donation** programme.
[0:5:3] # Trauma Registry (21 Oct 2026)
[0:5:4] - For timely treatment in the **Golden Hour**; **Pre-Hospital Notification** by **108 and 104** ambulance staff.
[0:5:5] - **Good Samaritan** protection: **Section 134A, Motor Vehicles Act, 1988**.
[0:5:6] # Artificial paneer ban (29 Oct 2026)
[0:5:7] - Gazette notification bans manufacture, storage, transport and sale of artificial paneer for **one year**.
[0:5:8] - **FSSAI:** set up under the **Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006**; under the Union **Health** Ministry.
[0:5:9] ~~ Sources: Praveen daily CA 21 Oct 2026 p.8 (Trauma Registry); Praveen daily CA 29 Oct 2026 p.5 (paneer ban); Praveen CA-360 Sep 2026 p.37 (organ transplantation); app notes 6:0 (sub 5)

## SUB 8 | Fisheries and Skill Development
[0:8:0] # Pulicat Lake – GPS fishing boundary
[0:8:1] - **Pulicat** = **brackish-water** lake shared by **AP and Tamil Nadu**.
[0:8:2] - Fishermen asked for **GPS transponders** on boats instead of **palmyrah trunk markers**; **NCCR (National Centre for Coastal Research)** to fix digital coordinates.
[0:8:3] - **Rayadoruvu sea mouth project:** **₹133.11 crore** from the Union Fisheries Ministry; about **199** cases pending against TN fishermen.
[0:8:4] # Apprenticeship bodies
[0:8:5] TABLE head: Body | Chair
  [0:8:5.0] State Apprenticeship Council | Principal Secretary, Skill Development & Training Dept
  [0:8:5.1] NAPS 2.0 State-Level Committee | Director of Employment and Training
[0:8:6] - **NAPS 2.0:** **Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship**; legal basis **Apprentices Act, 1961**.

## SUB 9 | New Current Affairs: 5–14 Nov 2026
[0:9:0] # Cabinet decisions (10 Nov 2026)
[0:9:1] - **State Cabinet, Tuesday 10 Nov 2026** (CM **N. Chandrababu Naidu**) cleared **30+ agenda items**: **AP AVGC-XR Policy 2026–31** (Animation, Visual Effects, Gaming, Comics and Extended Reality); **AP Robotics and Physical AI Policy 2026–2036**; draft amendments to the **AP Semiconductor and Display Fab Policy (4.0) 2024–29**.
[0:9:2] - **Directorate of Traffic Management and Road Safety:** a new directorate at **Police HQ, Mangalagiri**, headed by an **IGP**-rank officer (Home Department).
[0:9:3] - **APGENCO** allowed to explore **Small Modular Reactor (SMR)** nuclear technology as a future alternative to thermal units.
[0:9:4] - **FLO Women Industrial Park:** **30 acres** at **Jayanthipuram, NTR district**, at concessional price, for women entrepreneurs recommended by the **FICCI Ladies Organisation (FLO)**.
[0:9:5] # Appointments and statutory bodies
[0:9:6] - **APERC Chairperson (4 Nov 2026):** retired IAS officer **Binoy Kumar**, succeeding **P. Venkata Rama Reddy**; term **5 years or till age 70**, whichever is earlier; appointed under the **AP Electricity Reform Act, 1998** (he was Principal Secretary to the CM of undivided AP, 2012–13). **APERC** = statutory body set up **31 March 1999**; chairperson + **two members**; **Ancha Pandu Ranga Rao** = Member (Legal), Oct 2026.
[0:9:7] - **APHEFRMC (11 Nov 2026):** **P. Chandrasekhar** appointed **National Medical Commission (NMC)** representative on the **AP Higher Education Fee Regulatory and Monitoring Committee** for **3 years**; body set up **19 Aug 2019** under the **AP Higher Education Regulatory and Monitoring Commission Act, 2019** – regulates fees of **private unaided** higher education institutions, bars **capitation fees**.
[0:9:8] - **Vidya Vahini portal (11 Nov 2026):** launched by **APSCHE** on **National Education Day (11 Nov, birth anniversary of Maulana Abul Kalam Azad)** under its **LMS–MOOCs** initiative; first **three** courses – Introduction to AI, Applications of AI, **Quantum Technology** (each **4 credits**, 40 modules). **APSCHE**: est. **20 May 1988** under the **APSCHE Act, 1988 (Act 16 of 1988)**, on the **NPE 1986** advice – **India's first State Council of Higher Education**.
[0:9:9] - **Municipal office-bearers:** the **State Election Commission** fixed elections of **Deputy Mayors and Vice-Chairpersons** on **23 Nov 2026**. Under AP's amended municipal laws the **Mayor and Municipal Chairperson are directly elected** by voters, the **Deputy Mayor and Vice-Chairperson indirectly** by members; **Art. 243R(2)(b)** lets the State law decide how the chairperson is elected (**74th Amendment, 1992**; three types of municipalities under **Art. 243Q**).
[0:9:10] - **12th Pay Revision Commission:** **AP JAC Amaravati** (employees' body formed **5 Feb 2017, Tirupati**; chairman **Bopparaju Venkateswarlu**) asked the 12th PRC to report within **six months**.
[0:9:11] # e-Governance and services
[0:9:12] - **Mana Mitra** (Centre lauded its use in **cyclone management**, Nov 2026): **WhatsApp governance** service launched **30 Jan 2025** by IT Minister **Nara Lokesh** at **Undavalli**; WhatsApp number **95523 00009**; **161** services at launch → **200** by March 2025. Other AP e-governance tools: **D-Krishi (2017)** – subsidised seeds with Aadhaar authentication; **eHospital (2022)**; **e-Cabinet (2024)** – paperless Cabinet meetings.
[0:9:13] - **Ease of Vehicle Ownership Index (EVOI)** by **IndiaTech.org** with **Consumer VOICE**: **AP ranked 1st** for smooth used-vehicle ownership transfer, then **Kerala, Tamil Nadu**; Delhi 4th; **Uttar Pradesh last**. *(news 10 Nov 2026)*
[0:9:14] - **PHC patient-satisfaction ranking (Oct 2026)** by the Health Department, through **IVRS** feedback on four parameters (doctors present, cleanliness, money demanded, medicines): **Palnadu 1st (81.6%)**, Polavaram 81.5%, Nellore 80.7%; **Kurnool last (69.5%)**.
[0:9:15] # Disaster, drought and crop insurance
[0:9:16] - **Drought mandals, Kharif 2026 (G.O. 5):** **24 mandals in 8 districts** – **12 severe, 12 moderate**; issued by **G. Sai Prasad**, Special Chief Secretary, Revenue (Disaster Management). Severe: Racherla (Markapuram); Aspari, Nandavaram, Adoni-2, Kosigi, Mantralayam, Kollur (**Kurnool**); Rayadurg, Peddapappur (Anantapur); Rolla, Agali, Penukonda (Sri Sathya Sai).
[0:9:17] - **PMFBY, Kharif 2026:** after cyclones **Arnab** (crossed near **Narsapur**, 28 Oct) and **Murasu**, the CM said crop insurance must cover farmers **and tenants**; **TATA AIG General Insurance** selected for **nine districts** (Anakapalli, Bapatla, Chittoor, Konaseema, Krishna, Kurnool, NTR, Parvathipuram Manyam, SPSR Nellore); implemented by the **Agriculture and Cooperation Department**. PMFBY: launched **18 Feb 2016**; farmer premium **2% Kharif, 1.5% Rabi, 5%** commercial/horticultural crops.
[0:9:18] - **Hepatitis B and C:** over **10 lakh** people (over 2% of AP) estimated affected; a **three-dose Hepatitis B vaccination** drive for about **3.69 lakh** high-risk people; more cases in NTR, East Godavari, Visakhapatnam, Kurnool, Nellore. *(news 4 Nov 2026)*
[0:9:19] # Awards and rankings
[0:9:20] - **Swachh Survekshan 2025–26** (**10th edition**, MoHUA; theme "**Swachhata Ki Nayi Pehel – Badhayein Haath, Karein Safai Saath**"; awards given by President **Droupadi Murmu**): **six AP ULBs** awarded – GVMC, Vijayawada, Guntur, Tirupati, Rajamahendravaram, Kuppam. **Vijayawada** among the **top three of the Super Swachh League** (with Navi Mumbai, Surat); **Tirupati** in the Super Swachh League for the **4th straight year** and, with **Kuppam** (mentee), **3rd** in the **Swachh Shehar Jodi** category; **Visakhapatnam** among the top three in the **million-plus** category (with Jabalpur, Lucknow). Swachh Survekshan began **2016** under **SBM-U**.
[0:9:21] - **National SARAS Mela 2026** (**13–31 Oct 2026**, **Punnami Ghat, Vijayawada**) – the **first** National SARAS Mela held **outside Delhi-NCR**; by the **Ministry of Rural Development** with **SERP**; total sales **₹12.84 crore**; **AP 1st – ₹2.99 crore (~23%)**, Uttar Pradesh 2nd (₹1.22 crore). First SARAS Mela: **1999, Delhi**.
[0:9:22] > ⚠ Trap: Binoy Kumar heads **APERC** (not APSERC/APTRANSCO); Mayor is **directly** elected but the **Deputy Mayor indirectly**; Vidya Vahini is an **APSCHE** portal launched on **11 Nov (Azad's birthday)**, not Teachers' Day; 24 drought mandals = 12 severe + 12 moderate; AP topped the **EVOI**, not Kerala.

=== NOTES 6:1 | Jun–Aug 2026: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | Talliki Vandanam and School / Higher Education Decisions
[1:0:0] # Talliki Vandanam (Thalliki Vandanam) 2026
[1:0:1] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [1:0:1.0] Earlier name | Amma Vodi
  [1:0:1.1] Department | School Education Department
  [1:0:1.2] Benefit | ₹15,000 per child per year to the mother/guardian; ₹2,000 deducted for school maintenance, sanitation, hygiene
  [1:0:1.3] 2026 DBT | From 22 July 2026 – ₹13,000 credited (second year of the scheme)
  [1:0:1.4] Coverage | Class 1 to 12 (incl. Intermediate) – government, aided, private, residential schools
  [1:0:1.5] Type | Conditional cash transfer – 75% attendance
  [1:0:1.6] Aim | Reduce schooling cost → lower dropout, higher Gross Enrolment Ratio (GER) (APPSC Endowment PYQ) – not GDP, literacy or admissions in government schools only
[1:0:2] - Benefit goes to **every eligible child** (not one per family); extended to orphaned children; account must be Aadhaar-seeded and NPCI-mapped. **DBT** was launched nationally in **2013**.
[1:0:3] TABLE head: Criterion | Rule
  [1:0:3.0] Income | Rural below ₹10,000/month; urban below ₹12,000/month
  [1:0:3.1] Land | Wet up to 3 acres / dry up to 10 acres
  [1:0:3.2] Electricity | Below 300 units per month
  [1:0:3.3] Other | Rice Card mandatory; house below 1,000 sq ft
[1:0:4] - **Excluded:** **ITI and Polytechnic** students.
[1:0:5] # Prashast 2.0, PM SHRI, KGBVs, Canva
[1:0:6] - **Prashast 2.0** app: mandatory screening for **21 types of disabilities** in **all schools** (incl. private), 2026–27. **Part-I – class teachers, by September 2026**; Part-II – special educators and Bhavita Centre staff, by October 2026.
[1:0:7] - **AP ranked 1st** in utilisation of **PM SHRI** funds (reviewed by Nara Lokesh); PM SHRI = Prime Minister's **Schools for Rising India** – model **government** schools under NEP 2020 (not private).
[1:0:8] - **KGBVs** in AP: **352**; seats 75% SC/ST/OBC/minority, **25% BPL**.
[1:0:9] - **Canva MoU:** free Canva for Education in **four phases**.
[1:0:10] # Higher education and new schools in Amaravati
[1:0:11] - **AP Higher Education Fee Regulatory and Monitoring Committee:** Chairman **Dr. Justice V.R.K. Krupasagar** (former AP HC judge).
[1:0:12] - **Four higher-education Bills (Aug 2026):** the Regulatory Commission turned into a **fee-regulation committee**; less land for private universities.
[1:0:13] - **Bedford School (UK):** founded **1552 by King Edward VI**; Amaravati = first foreign educational institution in Amaravati and the school's **third international campus**; its first India campus is at **Mohali**.
[1:0:14] - **Abburaajupalem academy:** built by a farmers' group (DAV Amaravati Rythu Vidyalaya Vedantam Group, Thullur) – **not** under the AP School Education Corporation.
[1:0:15] ~~ Sources: CARE Jul 2026 p.38 (Talliki Vandanam 2026); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.11 (reduce financial barriers to education); AP Budget 2026-27 speech p.10; Praveen CA-360 Jun 2026 p.35-36 (Prashast, PM SHRI, Bedford, Canva); Praveen CA-360 Jul 2026 p.32 (Krupasagar); app notes 6:1 (sub 1)
## SUB 1 | APCNF and PM-Kisan–Annadata Sukhibhava
[1:1:0] # APCNF wins the 2026 Food Planet Prize
[1:1:1] - **APCNF = Andhra Pradesh Community-Managed Natural Farming** – called the world's largest agroecology programme by farmers enrolled (APPSC Polytechnic Lecturers PYQ); not "Centralised", "Cultivable" or plain "Community".
[1:1:2] TABLE head: Particular | Fact
  [1:1:2.0] Prize | 2026 Food Planet Prize – $1.5 million
  [1:1:2.1] Given by | Curt Bergfors Foundation, Sweden
  [1:1:2.2] Presented | Båstad, Sweden, 2 June 2026
  [1:1:2.3] Selection | 1,000+ nominations; longlist of 35; one of 4 finalists (not "selected from 35 nominations")
[1:1:3] - Launched **2016** by **Rythu Sadhikara Samstha (RySS)**, which implements it; input **Jeevamrutham**; replicated in **22 other States**; target **100% natural farming State by 2047**.
[1:1:4] # Annadata Sukhibhava – PM-Kisan
[1:1:5] - **₹20,000 a year per farmer = ₹14,000 State + ₹6,000 Centre (PM-KISAN)**, in **three instalments** (not two).
[1:1:6] - **First instalment (Kharif 2026): ₹7,000** (₹5,000 + ₹2,000); released by CM at **Linganguntla, Palnadu**.
[1:1:7] - Agriculture Minister: **Kinjarapu Atchannaidu**. Contrast: Telangana Rythu Bharosa **₹12,000 per acre**.
[1:1:8] ~~ Sources: CARE Jun 2026 p.17 (APCNF, Food Planet Prize); Praveen CA-360 Jun 2026 p.9 (2016, RySS, four finalists of 35); CARE Jun 2026 p.51 (Annadata Sukhibhava); app notes 6:1 (sub 2)
## SUB 2 | EAGLE, Lokayukta, Districts and Other Appointments
[1:2:0] # EAGLE – anti-narcotics force
[1:2:1] - **EAGLE = Elite Anti-Narcotics Group for Law Enforcement** – AP's special anti-drug task force (APPSC Endowment PYQ; not ADF, OCTOPUS or SANKALP); renamed EAGLE in **November 2024**; HQ **Mangalagiri**; head **IGP Aake Ravikrishna**; toll-free **1972**.

[1:2:3] - Chief Secretary **G. Sai Prasad** presented EAGLE at the national Narco-Coordination conference.
[1:2:4] - **Operation Chaitanyam:** ganja on 94.77 acres destroyed in **Alluri Sitarama Raju** district (not Prakasam).
[1:2:5] # Lokayukta and task forces
[1:2:6] - **AP Lokayukta:** Justice **Tallapragada Mallikarjuna Rao** (retd AP HC judge), **5 years**, picked by a committee headed by **CM Chandrababu Naidu**; laws: **AP Lokayukta and Upa-Lokayukta Act 1983** and **Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act 2013**.
[1:2:7] - **Bamboo task force:** chaired by the **Chief Secretary**.

  [1:2:9.2] Lead bank (RBI) | Union Bank of India for both

## SUB 3 | Weavers, Fishermen and BC Welfare
[1:3:0] # Netanna Sevalo (Nethannala Sevalo)
[1:3:1] - Launched on the **12th National Handloom Day, 7 August 2026**, in **Chirala constituency, Bapatla district**.
[1:3:2] - **₹25,000 a year** to **71,536** loom-owning families (₹179 crore).
[1:3:3] TABLE head: Weaver support | Detail
  [1:3:3.0] Free power – handloom | Up to 200 units/month, 62,541 families
  [1:3:3.1] NTR Pension Bharosa | Weavers get ₹4,000/month
[1:3:4] - **Handloom Park:** **Mangalagiri**; **Handloom and Handicrafts Museum:** **Shakhamuru, Amaravati**; **Sustainable Textile Recycling Unit:** **Visakhapatnam** (₹4,200 crore, Fabindia tie-up).
[1:3:5] - **Suryalanka–Chirala tourism corridor:** **₹97 crore** under Swadesh Darshan.
[1:3:6] # Fishermen
[1:3:7] - **Free satellite transponders** under **PM Matsya Sampada Yojana**, distributed at **Singarayakonda (Prakasam)** – for **weather/cyclone alerts, SOS, border-crossing warnings and faster rescue**.
[1:3:8] - Fishing-ban assistance **₹20,000**; pension from **50 years**; fisheries development boards at **Visakhapatnam and Bhimavaram**; modern fish market planned at Singarayakonda.

=== NOTES 6:2 | Mar–May 2026: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | Welfare Schemes, Renamed Schemes and P4 (Mar–May 2026)
[2:0:0] # Divyang Shakti – free bus travel
[2:0:1] - Inaugurated **18 March 2026** at **Mangalagiri (Guntur district)** by CM **N. Chandrababu Naidu**: **free travel on RTC buses** for persons with disabilities.
[2:0:2] - Eligibility: **more than 40% disability**; **21 categories**; **helpers may accompany** beneficiaries.
[2:0:3] - Buses: **City Ordinary, Metro Express, Palle Velugu, Ultra Palle Velugu, Express** – urban and rural (not AC/luxury services).
[2:0:4] - About **12.76 lakh** beneficiaries; about **₹207 crore** a year.
[2:0:5] # Disability framework and Akshaya Kshetram
[2:0:6] - **Akshaya Kshetram** (near Tirumala, Tirupati): residential home for persons with **intellectual disabilities**; founded **2 October 1996** as a **daycare centre**; completed **30 years** in 2026.
[2:0:7] TABLE head: Law / scheme | Fact
  [2:0:7.0] RPwD Act, 2016 | Disability categories 7 → 21
  [2:0:7.1] Accessible India Campaign | = Sugamya Bharat Abhiyan
  [2:0:7.2] DDRS | Deendayal Disabled Rehabilitation Scheme – funds NGOs
  [2:0:7.3] National Trust Act, 1999 | Four disabilities: autism, cerebral palsy, mental retardation, multiple disabilities (blindness is not one)
  [2:0:7.4] UNCRPD | India a signatory – rights-based approach
[2:0:8] # P4 Margadarsi–Bangaru Kutumbam – first anniversary
[2:0:9] - **P4 = Public-Private-People Partnership**; launched on **Ugadi, 30 March 2025** at Amaravati.
[2:0:10] - Model: the **top 10%** (**Margadarsi = mentor / donor**) support the **bottom 20%** (**Bangaru Kutumbam = beneficiary "golden family"**) – voluntary mentoring and aid, **not a government cash-transfer scheme**.
[2:0:11] - **First-anniversary event, 30 March 2026:** Sri Padmavati Mahila Viswa Vidyalayam, **Tirupati**; **1.06 lakh Margadarsis** adopted about **10.30 lakh families**; companies adopted **8,904 families in Kuppam** under CSR.
[2:0:12] - Goal restated: a **poverty-free society by 2047** (zero poverty was first targeted for 2029).
[2:0:13] # Schemes renamed after the 2024 change of government

## SUB 2 | Amaravati – the Capital Act 2026
[2:2:0] TABLE head: Year / date | Event
  [2:2:0.0] 2014 | AP Reorganisation Act (under Article 3); Section 5: Hyderabad = common capital for not more than 10 years from 2 June 2014 (till June 2024); it named no capital – only "there shall be a new capital"
  [2:2:0.1] 2019 | Three capitals proposed: Visakhapatnam (executive), Amaravati (legislative), Kurnool (judicial)
  [2:2:0.2] 2020 | AP Decentralisation and Inclusive Development of All Regions Act, 2020 – repealed in 2021; the judicial capital was never operationalised
  [2:2:0.3] 2022 | AP High Court: State lacked competence to shift the capital; legitimate expectation of land-pooling farmers
  [2:2:0.4] 26 Mar 2026 | Cabinet (CM Naidu) approved the resolution and 96 new courts (1,730 posts)
  [2:2:0.5] 28 Mar 2026 | AP Assembly resolution asking Parliament to amend Section 5
  [2:2:0.6] 1–2 Apr 2026 | Parliament passed the AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Bill, 2026 (LS 1 April, RS 2 April)
  [2:2:0.7] Assent | President Droupadi Murmu under Article 111; in force after Gazette notification
  [2:2:0.8] Effect | Retrospective from 2 June 2024
[2:2:1] - The 2026 Act amends **Section 5(2)**: inserts the words **"at Amaravati"** (not "at Visakhapatnam") → Amaravati is the **sole and permanent capital**; **future changes to the capital area need Parliament's approval**.
[2:2:2] - **Amaravati** = the area notified under the **APCRDA Act, 2014**.
[2:2:3] - The **Constitution does not specify** how a State capital is chosen – statute fills the gap.
[2:2:4] # Amaravati capital project
[2:2:5] - Planned area about **217 sq km**; land through the **Land Pooling Scheme** (farmers get developed plots + annuity).
[2:2:6] - APCRDA hired a consultant (**Mar–Apr 2026**) for a governance framework in line with the **73rd and 74th Amendments**.
[2:2:7] # National Judicial Academy and Justice City
[2:2:8] - CM Naidu urged the **Supreme Court** to set up the **National Judicial Academy** in Amaravati, at a mediation conference of the AP State Legal Services Authority; chief guest **CJI Justice Surya Kant**, who called **mediation a pillar of justice**.
[2:2:9] - **India's first Justice City** (capital region): High Court, NJA, Legal Services Authority, Mediation Centre, **International Law University**, Bar Council of India.
[2:2:10] ~~ Sources: Praveen CA-360 Apr 2026 p.28 (Section 5(2), Art 111, 28 Mar resolution, APCRDA Act area, Parliament approval); PMF LENS Apr 2026 Part 2 p.1 (sole and permanent capital; retrospective 2 June 2024); CARE Apr 2026 p.5 (Section 5, ten years from 2 June 2014), p.11 (217 sq km); CARE Mar 2026 p.2-3 (NJA, Justice City, CJI Surya Kant); CivicCentre APPSC daily 9 Nov 2026 p.4 (land pooling scheme); app notes 6:2 (sub 0: 96 courts, three-capital timeline, 73rd/74th)
## SUB 3 | Laws, Bills and Regulatory Reform
[2:3:0] TABLE head: Bill (Assembly, March 2026) | Key points
  [2:3:0.0] AP Public Services (Second Amendment) Bill, 2025 | Junior Assistant job on compassionate grounds to the son of Thota Chandraiah (victim of political violence, Palnadu); appointment by special resolution of the Council of Ministers, bypassing APPSC
  [2:3:0.1] AP Fire Service (Amendment) Bill, 2026 | NOC replaced by a Fire Safety Certificate (FSC); fire department designated an Emergency Service; aligns with the Centre's 2019 Model Bill; standard fine ₹500 → ₹10,000; daily default fine ₹100 → ₹1,000
  [2:3:0.2] AP Jan Vishwas (Amendment of Provisions) Bill, 2026 | Decriminalises minor offences (jail → fines); inspired by the Centre's Jan Vishwas Act, 2023; repeals drainage cess Acts of 1985 and 1986; motto "Minimum Government, Maximum Governance"
[2:3:1] - SC view: compassionate appointment is a **valid exception** to equality in public employment (**Article 16**).
[2:3:2] # Deregulation Phase-2
[2:3:3] - Regulations **800+ → fewer than 100**; clearance time cut by **at least 40%**; through the **Ordinance route**.
[2:3:4] - Self-declaration / intimation, merged approvals, **end of double licensing**, third-party inspections, **colonial laws removed** (not retained); safety safeguards kept.
[2:3:5] - Digital tool **CFMS = Comprehensive Financial Management System**; **Visakhapatnam** = AP's largest port by cargo.
[2:3:6] - CM **N. Chandrababu Naidu** received **"Business Reformer of the Year"** (**The Economic Times**, Mumbai).
[2:3:7] # AP Electronic Processes Rules, 2025 (e-summons)
[2:3:8] - **AP Electronic Processes (Issuance, Service and Execution) Rules, 2025** – made by the **AP High Court**; G.O.Ms.No.107, Home (Courts-B), 22 May 2026.
[2:3:9] - Summons, warrants, notices via **WhatsApp, Signal, Telegram and email**, encrypted and **digitally signed** by the judicial officer; police submit a **screenshot or delivery report** as proof; **physical service continues where e-service fails** (it is not discontinued).
[2:3:10] - Background: the **IT Act, 2000** recognises electronic records and signatures; during **COVID-19** the SC allowed service by email, WhatsApp, Telegram, Signal.
[2:3:11] ~~ Sources: CARE Mar 2026 p.6 (Thota Chandraiah, Junior Assistant, special resolution, Art 16), p.7-8 (FSC, fines, Jan Vishwas 2023, drainage cess, motto); CARE Apr 2026 p.32-33 (Deregulation Phase-2, 800 → under 100, 40%, CFMS); Praveen CA-360 Apr 2026 p.30 (Business Reformer of the Year); app notes 6:2 (sub 3: e-summons rules, Ordinance route)

## SUB 5 | Draft Population Management Policy 2026
[2:5:0] - Introduced by CM **N. Chandrababu Naidu** – **first of its kind in India**; shift from population **control** to population **management** (pro-natalist).
[2:5:1] - Objective: raise TFR to **replacement level 2.1**; AP fertility about **1.5**, projected **1.2 by 2040**; **SRS 2024: AP TFR 1.4** (not 1.6); India 1.9.
[2:5:2] - AP **median age 32.5 years** (India 28.4); **female LFPR target ~31% → nearly 59%**.

  [2:5:3.2] Maternity leave | 12 months for the third child
  [2:5:3.3] Paternity leave | 2 months
  [2:5:3.4] IVF for childless couples | Through a public-private partnership

## SUB 6 | Urban, Transport, Water and Energy
[2:6:0] # Operation Clean Sweep (23 May – 20 June 2026)
[2:6:1] - In all ULBs under **Swarna Andhra–Swachh Andhra (SASA)**; based on the **Jaipur model**; geo-tagged daily reporting.
[2:6:2] TABLE head: Week | Focus
  [2:6:2.0] 1 | Garbage black spots, roads
  [2:6:2.1] 2 | Desilting storm water drains, nallas, backlanes
  [2:6:2.2] 3 | Source segregation, bulk waste generators
  [2:6:2.3] 4 | Public toilets, garbage vulnerable points
[2:6:3] # APSRTC electric fleet
[2:6:4] - Approved **5,746 electric buses + 500 CNG** over **5 years**; running cost **30–40% lower** than diesel; LFP batteries, range **150–300 km**.
[2:6:5] - **Gross Cost Contract**: the **private operator owns and maintains** the buses; govt pays per km. GST on EVs **5%**.
[2:6:6] - **PM2.5** = fine particulate pollutant (not an e-mobility programme); support via **FAME India II, PM e-Bus Sewa**.
[2:6:7] # PM Surya Ghar Utsav (Banaganapalle, Nandyal)
[2:6:8] - **100% subsidy** for rooftop solar to **SC and ST** households; up to **300 units free**; national target **1 crore households** (not 10 crore), under **MNRE**.
[2:6:9] TABLE head: Scheme | Focus
  [2:6:9.0] UDAY | State takeover of DISCOM debt
  [2:6:9.1] YSR Jala Kala | Free borewells for SC/ST and small farmers
  [2:6:9.2] PM-KUSUM | Solar pumps for farmers
[2:6:10] # Jal Jeevan Mission 2.0 MoU
[2:6:11] - AP MoU: safe drinking water to every household by **2028**; JJM (launched 2019; 16 crore households in Phase I) **extended till 2028**; JJM 2.0 shifts from infrastructure to **sustainability**.
[2:6:12] - O&M policy: maintenance by **Panchayats**; DWCRA women test water quality.
[2:6:13] # Policing and disaster technology
[2:6:14] - **Nellore AI ITMS**: detects **red-light jumping, stop-line crossing**, over-speeding by **radar** (ANPR reads number plates, linked to **VAHAN**); **VAHAN = vehicle registration**, **SARATHI = driving licences**.
[2:6:15] - **AP Cyber Guard**: respond to **1930** complaints within the **first 30 minutes**; **24×7 Cyber War Room**; training for **500** police; 1930 helpline under **I4C** (Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre, 2018) – not the NBA.
[2:6:16] - **NRSC–APSDMA MoU**: remote sensing to map **lightning hotspots**; NRSC est. **1974**, Hyderabad (ISRO).

[2:9:1] - **Farm to Home**: farmers to consumers via FPOs, linked with **Rythu App, Digi Rythu Bazaar App and Rythu Bazaars** (not Rythu Bazaars alone).
[2:9:2] - **Eastern Ghats seed network**: **Gasaba village, Peda Bayalu mandal, Alluri Sitharama Raju district**; **Soil and Seed Information Centre, Kiloguda** – 2001 with 45 varieties → **360+** now.
[2:9:3] - **Araku Kouni**: AP's first **tribal millet menu** – recipes of **Alluri Sitarama Raju** district; first in **Haritha restaurants (APTDC) in ASR district**; documented by **Indian Culinary Institute, Tirupati**.
[2:9:4] # Livestock
[2:9:5] - **FMD vaccination**: **16 March – 29 April 2026** under NADCP; free, door-to-door; calves above **four months**. FMD is a **viral** disease.
[2:9:6] - **Heat-stress advisory**: **clean drinking water through the day**, **community water troughs** in villages, sheds **east–west**; **crossbred / high-yield animals are more vulnerable** than indigenous breeds. **India** has the world's largest cattle population.
[2:9:7] - **Pashu Samvardhaka Margadarshini**: **livestock e-magazine** launched by Minister **K. Atchannaidu**.

=== NOTES 6:3 | Dec 2025–Feb 2026: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | Cabinet Decisions, Collectors' Conference and Welfare Delivery (Dec 2025 – Feb 2026)
[3:0:0] # Cabinet of 11 February 2026 (20 proposals)
[3:0:1] - Approved **"AP AI Living Labs"**: the **AI Living Labs Foundation** – a **Section-8 non-profit company**, Amaravati; AI training to **at least one person per family**; provides **GPUs and datasets** (it does not work without them); free AI teaching kits.
[3:0:2] - **State Data Centre** at the **IIIT Tirupati campus** (with disaster recovery), on the **BOOT** model.
[3:0:3] - **House regularisation on government land** extended to **30 June 2026**; **7,189 water management works**, ₹344 crore.
[3:0:4] - Draft bills: **Assigned Lands Amendment Bill–2026** and **Gram/Ward Secretariat Bill–2026**.
[3:0:5] # Digital / biometric attendance for MLAs
[3:0:6] - Decided by Assembly **Speaker Chintakayala Ayyanna Patrudu**: **biometric attendance for MLAs introduced in February 2026** (from the Budget Session), after YSRCP MLAs (11) stayed away.
[3:0:7] - **Article 190**: a seat may be declared vacant if a member is absent **60 days without permission**.
[3:0:8] # Collectors' Conference (December 2025)
[3:0:9] - **AP Population Management Policy** to be introduced **by Ugadi 2026**; aim: an **ageing population** (not over-population); promote normal deliveries.
[3:0:10] - **Family Cards**: **digitally integrate details of all welfare beneficiaries**.
[3:0:11] - Jobs: **20 lakh promised (Yuva Galam)**; **4.5 lakh created**. Collectors' goals: **zero maternal mortality, zero infant mortality, zero suicides**.
[3:0:12] - **Nutrition plan** for **Mid-Day Meal, Anganwadi and Anna Canteen** beneficiaries.
[3:0:13] - **Groundwater targets**: raise to **3 metres** below ground at the end of the monsoon; not to fall below **8 metres** before the next monsoon (8 m is the floor, not the monsoon-end target).
[3:0:14] - Swarnandhra-2047 has **10** guiding principles (Padi Sutralu), not 5; aim to **double GSDP and per capita income every five years** (not ten).
[3:0:15] # Zero excise on raw tobacco
[3:0:16] - Excise on **unbranded/raw tobacco** (HS code **2401**) cut to **0%** from **1 February 2026**; notified by the **Finance Ministry (CBIC)**, not the Agriculture Ministry.
[3:0:17] - Aim: **remove classification ambiguity and curb evasion**; CM Naidu **welcomed it** as a benefit to AP tobacco farmers.
[3:0:18] # AP 2025 review (Lanka Dinakar, 20-Point Programme)
[3:0:19] TABLE head: Item | Detail
  [3:0:19.0] Amaravati capital works | ₹15,000 crore central assistance
  [3:0:19.1] Polavaram | nearly ₹18,000 crore
  [3:0:19.2] Investments approved in 2025 | ₹8.55 lakh crore → 8.23 lakh jobs
  [3:0:19.3] Veligonda Project (Phase-I) | nearing completion – for drought-prone regions (not the ₹15,000 crore item)
  [3:0:19.4] CII Partnership Summit | Visakhapatnam – MoUs ₹13.25 lakh crore
  [3:0:19.5] Farmer transfers | ₹6,310 crore to 46 lakh farmers
  [3:0:19.6] Welfare disbursement | ₹10,090 crore to 67.27 lakh beneficiaries
  [3:0:19.7] Deepam | three free LPG cylinders to poor families
  [3:0:19.8] Stree Shakti | free bus travel for women (not LPG)
[3:0:20] - The review placed AP's growth against **India as the world's fourth-largest economy**.

## SUB 2 | Policing: AI4AP, Enforcement Drives and Women's Safety
[3:2:0] # AI4AP Police project
[3:2:1] - **Pilot in Chittoor, Guntur and Annamayya** (February 2026); launched by DGP **Harish Kumar Gupta** at **Mangalagiri Police HQ**; integrated with **CCTNS**; AI **assists, not replaces** investigators.
[3:2:2] - Origin: **AI4 Andhra Police Hackathon (June 2025)** – India's first police-specific AI hackathon; **knowledge partner 4sightAI**, technology partner Sparity.
[3:2:3] - **Eight modules**: complaint reception, digital evidence, social media monitoring, petition review, telecom/banking notices, investigation co-pilot, OCR for paper records, news tracking – **no facial-recognition training module**.
[3:2:4] # Enforcement drives
[3:2:5] TABLE head: Drive | Key facts
  [3:2:5.0] Operation Vajra Prahar (SPS Nellore) | Anti-drug cycle rally, slogan "Say No to Drugs, Bro – Ride a Cycle, Bro"; habitual offenders detained under the PIT NDPS Act; State anti-narcotics wing AP EAGLE
  [3:2:5.1] Operation Chinnari-Thalli (statewide, AP Police Women and Child Safety Wing) | Crimes against children; tagline "Safe childhood for sustainable development"; Sexual Offenders Sheets for offenders of the past 5 years; charge sheets within 60 days; SPs/CPs to prepare action plans within 48 hours (not 24)
[3:2:6] # Visakhapatnam and women's safety
[3:2:7] - **Visakhapatnam** projected by AP as the **"Safest City for Women"** after the **NARI 2025** report (NCW, Aug 2025) put it among the top safest cities (with Kohima, Bhubaneswar); measures: **Shakti Teams and the Shakti App**; police reach time **15 minutes urban**, 10 minutes rural.
[3:2:8] # Police health insurance (Visakhapatnam City Police)
[3:2:9] - Described as the **first of its kind** for police in India. Cover: **below 55 years ₹2 lakh – ₹15 lakh**; above 55 years ₹3 lakh – ₹30 lakh; **first-year premium paid by the police (police fund)**; covers **spouse + 2 children**; **2,576 families** in year one.
[3:2:10] ~~ Sources: CARE Feb 2026 p.7-8 (AI4AP pilot districts, CCTNS, hackathon), p.26 (Vajra Prahar, NDPS), p.32-33 (Chinnari-Thalli); India TV News 28 Aug 2025 and Outlook coverage of NARI 2025 (search results: Kohima, Visakhapatnam, Bhubaneswar among safest); The Hans India 5 Dec 2025 "Adequate health insurance coverage for police" (fetched: Visakhapatnam City Police, 2,576 families, first-year premium from police fund); app notes 6:3 (sub 3: modules, 48 hours, insurance age bands)

=== NOTES 6:4 | Apr–Nov 2025: Andhra Pradesh - government decisions, new schemes, appointments and administrative changes
## SUB 0 | Health - Universal Health Policy, NTR Vaidya Seva and Gates Partnership
[4:0:0] # Universal Health Policy (Cabinet, September 2025)
[4:0:1] - Approved by the AP Cabinet chaired by CM N. Chandrababu Naidu.
[4:0:2] - Free treatment **up to ₹25 lakh per family per year**, **irrespective of economic status**: AP is the **first State** to offer such high-value universal cover.
[4:0:3] - Beneficiaries: **1.63 crore families** = **1.43 crore economically weaker + 20 lakh others**.
[4:0:4] - Integrates **Ayushman Bharat + NTR Vaidya Seva**; network of **2,493 hospitals**; **3,257 procedures**.
[4:0:5] - **Pre-authorisation within 6 hours**.
[4:0:6] TABLE head: Layer (hybrid model) | Cover | Paid by
  [4:0:6.0] Insurance | claims up to ₹2.5 lakh per family | insurance companies
  [4:0:6.1] Trust | claims ₹2.5 lakh – ₹25 lakh per family | NTR Vaidya Seva Trust
[4:0:7] - The **hybrid model** was announced in **January 2025**: insurance ₹2.5 lakh plus Trust-funded treatment up to **₹25 lakh per family** for the poor (**APPSC 2025 key: ₹25 lakh**).
[4:0:8] - **Insurers:** the insurance layer uses **both public and private insurance companies**; the Trust bears ₹2.5–25 lakh (APPSC FRO 2025: both statements – ₹25 lakh per family a year and public + private insurers – correct).
[4:0:9] - **APSES 2024–25** (earlier pattern): up to **₹5 lakh per family per year**; total up to ₹25 lakh; **no limit for cancer**; annual family income up to **₹5 lakh**; runs alongside **Ayushman Bharat PM-JAY (₹5 lakh per family)**.
[4:0:10] > Insurance handles the first ₹2.5 lakh; the Trust handles ₹2.5–25 lakh, not the reverse.
[4:0:11] > ₹2.5 lakh = insurance layer; ₹25 lakh = per family (Trust); ₹5 lakh = per family a year (PM-JAY / APSES).
[4:0:12] # NTR Vaidya Seva - dues and rationalisation
[4:0:13] - **ASHA (AP Speciality Hospitals Association)** resumed **cashless services from 8 April 2025** after the CM released **₹500 crore**.
[4:0:14] - Suspension was in **over 730 private hospitals**; dues **₹3,500 crore** (**₹2,000 crore** to private hospitals); **package rates unchanged since 2018**.
[4:0:15] - Scheme: free tertiary care for BPL patients through empanelled hospitals.
[4:0:16] - **Rationalisation:** **155 treatments added** to government hospitals (hysterectomy, appendectomy, ENT) → total **324**; **197 rare services continue free** (e.g. thymus removal, duodenum excision; not merged); earlier list inflated to **3,257** services (dengue, typhoid, paratyphoid listed separately); **319 similar services → 133 categories**; remaining **2,736 → 2,550 categories** via insurance company.
[4:0:17] # Project Sanjeevani
[4:0:18] - Launched by the CM at the **Swasth Nari–Sashakt Parivar Abhiyan** programme, **Visakhapatnam**; upcoming initiative for **preventive healthcare**.
[4:0:19] - Integrates **Ayushman Bharat + NTR health services + global practices**.
[4:0:20] - Partners: **Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation** and **TCS**: digital health records for about **5 crore citizens**.
[4:0:21] - Health budget **₹19,264 crore (2024–25)**; universal insurance **₹2.5 lakh per family** (not ₹10 lakh); NTR Vaidya Seva Trust up to ₹25 lakh for the poor.
[4:0:22] - **13,944 health camps** (17 Sept – 2 Oct): BP, diabetes, oral/breast/cervical cancer, TB.
[4:0:23] - Also: **Deepam** 3 free LPG cylinders a year; **DWCRA** groups ₹20,000 crore savings, default **2%**; **National Nutrition Month** (17 Sept – 16 Oct).
[4:0:24] # 104 Mobile Medical Unit (restructured)
[4:0:25] - **47 confirmatory tests at the doorstep** = **20 for all** (blood glucose, CBP, renal, liver, lipid profile, urine, arterial blood gas) + **27 disease-specific**; builds **Electronic Health Records**.
[4:0:26] - Provider: consortium led by **Bhavya Health Services Pvt Ltd** with **SRIT India Pvt Ltd**.
[4:0:27] - **₹195 per person** for 20 common tests; each vehicle screens **35 persons a day**; began on a **pilot basis**.
[4:0:28] # Joint decoy operation against female foeticide
[4:0:29] - **Karnataka and AP Health Departments**, **21 September 2025**, private nursing home in **Kodumuru taluk, Kurnool**.
[4:0:30] TABLE head: Law | Provision
  [4:0:30.0] PCPNDT Act, 1994 (amended 2003) | bans sex determination; up to 3 years jail, fine up to ₹50,000
  [4:0:30.1] MTP Act, 1971 (amended 2021) | up to 24 weeks in certain cases; not for sex selection
[4:0:31] - **NFHS-5 SRB: 929** girls per 1,000 boys; **Beti Bachao Beti Padhao (2015)**.
[4:0:32] # Gates Foundation partnership (2025)
[4:0:33] - **Bill & Melinda Gates Foundation:** AP's 2025 strategic partnership/MoU to improve **health, education and agriculture using technology** (AI, data, digital public infrastructure) (APPSC FSO 2025 key; not WHO, NITI Aayog or Tata Trusts).
[4:0:34] > Gates Foundation appears twice: the 2025 tech MoU (health, education, agriculture) and Project Sanjeevani (with TCS).
[4:0:35] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.225-227; CARE APPSC Feb 2026 p.19-21; biospectrumindia.com (Universal Health Policy, Sep 2025); app notes 6:4 (sub 1)

[4:1:15] - Cabinet **discontinued Mobile Dispensing Units (MDUs)**; **Fair Price Shops restored from 1 June 2025**.
[4:1:16] - IVRS feedback: **25%** beneficiaries not getting rations; **26%** MDUs overcharging; **288** diversion cases; **570** MDU vacancies.
[4:1:17] - **Doorstep delivery continues** for **senior citizens (65+)** and **persons with disabilities**.
[4:1:18] - MDU vehicles: free ownership to **SC, ST, BC, EBC** operators who paid 10% of cost.
[4:1:19] - **Six rice card services** at Village/Ward Secretariats: **new card, bifurcation, change of address, addition of members, removal of members, surrender**.
[4:1:20] - **WhatsApp Governance**: from **15 May**, message "Hello" to **95523 00009**.
[4:1:21] - **Smart Rice Cards** from **June 2025** for those completing eKYC; free.
[4:1:22] - **First time rice cards for transgender persons**; also single persons over 50, separated spouses, orphans, elderly in care homes.
[4:1:23] - **eKYC exempt**: children **below 5**, seniors **above 80**.
[4:1:24] - **AAY cards (35 kg rice)**: **Chenchus, Yanadis, pensioned rural artists**.
[4:1:25] # Other welfare and service schemes

[4:1:32] - **Pawan Kalyan** - **Adavi Thalli Baata**.
[4:1:33] # CMEGP vs PMEGP
[4:1:34] - **Chief Minister Employment Generation Programme (CMEGP)**: self-employment through micro and small enterprises, rural and urban; by **Department of MSME** through **District Industries Centres (DICs)**; modelled on **PMEGP**; for **first-generation entrepreneurs**; target **5,000–6,000 units a year**; plus a dedicated policy for **Common Facility Centres (CFCs)** for MSME clusters.
[4:1:35] TABLE head: Unit | CMEGP ceiling | PMEGP ceiling
  [4:1:35.0] Manufacturing | ₹75 lakh | ₹50 lakh
  [4:1:35.1] Service | ₹25 lakh | ₹20 lakh
[4:1:36] - **PMEGP**: **Central Sector Scheme (2008)**; above **18 years**, **no income ceiling**; **Class VIII pass** for projects above ₹10 lakh (manufacturing) / ₹5 lakh (service); subsidy **15%–35%**; implemented by **KVIC** (statutory body, **KVIC Act, 1956**).
[4:1:37] - **NTR Bharosa pension rates (from July 2024):** **₹4,000** a month for the aged, widows, weavers, single women, fishermen and transgender persons; **₹6,000** for persons with disabilities; **₹15,000** for the fully disabled; **₹10,000** for dialysis (kidney) patients. The first enhanced payment (1 July 2024) went to about **65 lakh** beneficiaries.
[4:1:38] - **Spouse pensions (May 2025):** **71,380** spouses of deceased pensioners were approved under the **spouse category**; each gets **₹4,000** a month, from the month after the pensioner's death.
[4:1:39] ~~ Sources: AP Budget 2026-27 speech p.24 (Stree Shakti from 15 Aug 2025); CARE APPSC Apr 2026 p.54-58; freepressjournal.in (Pink Saheli card); app notes 6:4 (sub 2)
## SUB 2 | P4 - Zero Poverty Programme
[4:2:0] # What P4 is
[4:2:1] - **P4 = Public-Private-People Partnership** (also written **People–Private–Public Partnership for the Poor**).
[4:2:2] - Launched on **Ugadi, 30 March 2025**; under the **Planning Department**; goal: **complete eradication of poverty** (**Zero Poverty** sutram); linked with **SDG 1 (No Poverty)**.
[4:2:3] - **Swarna Andhra P4 Foundation**: poverty eradication, under the **Planning Department**.
[4:2:4] - Claimed as a **first-of-its-kind convergence model** in India; government acts only as a **facilitator**; whole-of-society, **voluntary** model (2025-26).
[4:2:5] # Who is who in P4
[4:2:6] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [4:2:6.0] Margadarshi / Margadarsi | well-off mentor who adopts a family; selected voluntarily (no compulsion)
  [4:2:6.1] Bangaru Kutumbam / Bangaru Kutumbalu | "Golden Families": the poor families who are adopted
[4:2:7] - Mentor role goes beyond money: **emotional reassurance, guidance, continuous support**. Mentors can be individuals, NGOs or corporates.
[4:2:8] - Target: **5 lakh** Golden Families adopted **by 15 August**; Phase 1 scale about **21.8 lakh families**.
[4:2:9] - Donor platforms: **Milaap, DonateKart, RangDe**.
[4:2:10] - **Dokka Seethamma** (19th-century social reformer, known for selfless service to the poor) is remembered in P4 messaging.
[4:2:11] - Update (6 Jul 2026): about **9,000 Kuppam families** adopted by **Adani, TVS Motor and MEIL**.
[4:2:12] > Margadarsi = mentor (adopts); Bangaru Kutumbam = poor family (adopted). Margadarsis are volunteers, not nominated by government.
[4:2:13] # P4 structure
[4:2:14] TABLE head: Level | Head
  [4:2:14.0] State society | Chairperson: CM N. Chandrababu Naidu; Vice-Chairperson: Deputy CM Pawan Kalyan; CEO, Director, call centre
  [4:2:14.1] District | District In-charge Ministers
  [4:2:14.2] Constituency | MLAs
  [4:2:14.3] Village/ward | Panchayat / Ward Administrative Secretaries
[4:2:15] > "Primary goal of P4" = poverty eradication (zero poverty), SDG 1.
[4:2:16] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.301-302 (P4, zero poverty); app notes 6:4 (sub 0)
## SUB 3 | Laws, Bills and Cabinet Decisions
[4:3:0] # Sub-classification of Scheduled Castes
[4:3:1] - Bill sub-classifies AP's **59 Scheduled Castes** into **three groups** for reservation in education and public jobs.
[4:3:2] - **Ordinance** by the Governor on **16 April 2025**; based on the **One-Man Commission of Rajiv Ranjan Mishra** (IAS retd.) under the **Commission of Inquiry Act**.
[4:3:3]   - Commission set up by **G.O.Ms.No.86 (15 Nov 2024)** under the **Commission of Inquiry Act, 1952**; report **10 March 2025**; Mishra is a **retired IAS** officer (APPSC FSO 2025 key).
[4:3:4] - **Supreme Court, 1 August 2024**: **7-judge bench** upheld sub-classification by **6:1**.
[4:3:5] - SC reservation (central) **15%**; Articles **14–16**; DPSPs **38, 39, 41, 46**.
[4:3:6] # Monsoon Session 2025 - Bills
[4:3:7] TABLE head: Bill passed | Key point
  [4:3:7.0] AP Vehicles Taxation (Amendment) Bill, 2025 | green tax on commercial vehicles: 7–12 years ₹1,500/yr; over 12 years ₹3,000/yr (earlier quarterly slabs up to ₹20,000); replaces July 2025 ordinance
  [4:3:7.1] AP Shops and Establishments (Amendment) Bill, 2025 | working conditions, registration
  [4:3:7.2] AP State Commission for Scheduled Tribes (Amendment) Bill, 2025 | strengthens ST Commission
  [4:3:7.3] AP Prevention of Begging (Amendment) Bill, 2025 | rehabilitation
  [4:3:7.4] Factories (AP Amendment) Bill, 2025 | longer daily working hours within rest/spread-over limits; safeguards for women in night shifts
[4:3:8] - The 5 September Cabinet note described the green tax as **₹1,500 for tractors** and **₹3,000 for trailers**.
[4:3:9] - **Withdrawn (2019 Bills)**: Factories (AP Amendment) Bill 2019; Industrial Disputes (AP Amendment) Bill 2019; Labour Laws (AP Amendment for Compounding of Offences) Bill 2019.
[4:3:10] - Cabinet approved **13 new Bills** for tabling.
[4:3:11] # Building Penalisation Scheme (BPS) 2025
[4:3:12] - Notified via **G.O. Ms. 225** (MA&UD); regularises unauthorised constructions from **1 January 1985 to 31 August 2025**; final one-time chance; replaces **BPS 2019** (covered 1985–2018; voluntary, low participation).
[4:3:13] - **UCIMS portal** (Urban Construction Information Management System): **59,041** unauthorised buildings (31 Aug 2025); about **50,000 deviations** and **10,000 additional floors**.
[4:3:14] - Demand from **CREDAI, NAREDCO, CLESA** (and Vijayawada Municipal Corporation).
[4:3:15] - Coverage: Municipal Corporations, Municipalities, Nagar Panchayats, Gram Panchayats under UDAs; **APCRDA, VMRDA, VK–PCPIR UDA, IALAs**. **Capital City Area of APCRDA excluded**.
[4:3:16] TABLE head: Charge | Amount
  [4:3:16.0] Window | online, 120 days
  [4:3:16.1] Initial fee | ₹10,000 per application
  [4:3:16.2] Penalisation | ₹40–₹400 per sq ft
  [4:3:16.3] Concession | 25% pre-1997 buildings; 50% residential buildings in notified slums
[4:3:17] - Excluded: **public land**, environmentally restricted zones, disputed ownership, constructions after 31 Aug 2025.
[4:3:18] TABLE head: Act amended by BPS Bill | Year
  [4:3:18.0] Municipal Corporations Act | 1955
  [4:3:18.1] AP Municipalities Act | 1965
  [4:3:18.2] APCRDA Act | 2014
  [4:3:18.3] AP Metropolitan Region and UDA Act | 2016
  [4:3:18.4] Abbreviation | Full form

## SUB 4 | Police, Security and Excise
[4:4:0] # Madvi Hidma killed (18 November 2025)
[4:4:1] - **Madvi Hidma**, top **CPI (Maoist)** commander, **head of the Central Military Commission (CMC)**, killed early on **18 November 2025** (6–7 a.m.) near **Nellooru village, Maredumilli Mandal, ASR district**, near the AP–Chhattisgarh–Telangana tri-junction (Karegutta hills area), by the **Special Party team of ASR district police**; wife **Madkam Raje (Rajakka)** and four others also killed.
[4:4:2] - Born **Puvarti/Purvati, Sukma** (then undivided MP, now Chhattisgarh); tribal; **Bal Sangham** early 1990s → commander, **PLGA Battalion No. 1** → head of CMC.
[4:4:3] - **Youngest and only tribal Central Committee member from Bastar**; reward **₹50 lakh** (not ₹10 lakh); about **26 major attacks**.
[4:4:4] TABLE head: Year | Attack
  [4:4:4.0] 2010 | Dantewada (Tadmetla): about 76 CRPF killed
  [4:4:4.1] 2013 | Jhiram Ghati: Congress leaders killed
  [4:4:4.2] 2017 | Sukma / Burkapal: 26 CRPF killed
  [4:4:4.3] 2020 | Minpa–Burkapal ambushes
  [4:4:4.4] 2021 | Tekulgudem–Pedagelur: 22 security personnel killed
[4:4:5] - **Operation Kagar (2025)**: Union-led, multi-state anti-Maoist offensive; target to **end Maoism by March 2026**.
[4:4:6] - **Basavaraju (Nambala Keshava Rao)**, former General Secretary, killed **May 2025**; Central Committee shrank from about **42 (2004)** to about **12 (2025)**.
[4:4:7] - **PLGA = People's Liberation Guerrilla Army**, armed wing of CPI (Maoist).

Rajashekhar Babu** (IG rank; took charge 14 July 2023); **4,000+** cameras since August 2023, plus **2,000 matrix** and **300 facial recognition** cameras; linked to the **Central Command Control Centre, Vijayawada**; over **80%** crimes solved with CCTV; target **10,000** cameras within a year; only **500 of 1,371** places of worship covered.

No. 376, 15 October 2025**; every bottle's QR scanned before sale; entry in the **DLGVR** register; only **APSBCL** liquor allowed; **licensees personally responsible**; checks seal, cap, hologram; real-time Excise ERP; **24×7 Excise Control Room** and WhatsApp helpline.
[4:4:19] - **Annamayya district declared illicit liquor-free** on **2 September 2025** under **Navodayam 2.0** (a **State** programme, not Central/NPDDR): **71 villages**; **650 offenders** identified, **494 rehabilitated**; **325** given loans, **125** DRDA support; SDGs **3 and 16**.

By November 2025, **₹14,000 paid in two instalments** to **46.5 lakh farmers**; **₹6,310 crore** disbursed.

[4:5:6]   - Officials and scientists go to villages to make farmers aware of **low-cost, high-yield farming**; it is not an investment, volunteering or farm-tourism scheme. Also one of the **five campaigns** of the 2014 mission strategy (see 6:5).

[4:5:18] TABLE head: Animal | Cover | Premium
  [4:5:18.0] Cattle, buffaloes | 3 years | 6.40%
  [4:5:18.1] Sheep, goats, pigs | 1 year | 3%

[4:5:19] - In force from **1 April 2025**. **Farmer pays 15%** of premium; remaining **85% shared Centre:State 60:40** (AP is a general-category State).
[4:5:20] - Under the amended NLM norms (Cabinet, **February 2024**) the beneficiary share was cut to **15%** (earlier 20%–50%); the rest is shared **60:40** in general States, **90:10** in hilly and North-Eastern States, and **100%** by the Centre in UTs.

[4:5:23] # Fisheries and aquaculture
[4:5:24] - **'Super GST – Super Savings'**: AP Fisheries Department programme at **Narendrapuram** village; GST on key fisheries items cut **12% → 5%** (marine products, processed fish, shrimp, fish oils, organic manure, aquaculture equipment, fishing gear, ammonia and micronutrients, processing services).
[4:5:25] - **Power subsidy** **₹1.50 per unit** for aqua farmers registered under **APSADA**; average saving **₹20,000 per acre**.
[4:5:26] - **Union Budget 2025–26 reduced** (not increased) customs duty on shrimp feed, fish feed and aquaculture inputs.
[4:5:27] - **AIC–APGB MoU** (Guntur): **Agriculture Insurance Company of India** + **Andhra Pradesh Grameena Bank**; **bancassurance** (AIC products via APGB rural branches); covers shrimp losses from diseases, cyclones, poor water quality; livestock insurance planned. **APGB** = RRB sponsored by **Union Bank of India**.
[4:5:28] - AP produces **35–40% of India's shrimp**.
[4:5:29] - **APSADA Act, 2020 amendment**: licence validity **lifetime → 1 year** for aquaculture business (hatcheries, feed mills, seed units) and **5 years** for farming; aligns with **Coastal Aquaculture Authority Act, 2005**; old registrations endorsed on licence fee + **50% late fee** (about 20% farmers can regularise). **APSADA = Andhra Pradesh State Aquaculture Development Authority (Agency)**.
[4:5:30] - **Australia's first import approval for unpeeled Indian prawns** (earlier ban due to **White Spot Syndrome Virus**), announced by **Nara Lokesh** on his Australia tour (October 2025); the US had imposed steep tariffs on Indian prawns.

## SUB 6 | Education and APPSC
[4:6:0] # Personalised Adaptive Learning (PAL)
[4:6:1] - Under **Samagra Shiksha, AP**: **AP first State to embed PAL in the government school curriculum**.
[4:6:2] - **60 schools (2018) → 1,224 schools** (3.25 lakh students) in all 26 districts; adding **232 PM SHRI** schools (2025–26).
[4:6:3] - Result: **1.9 extra years of learning in 17 months** (**University of Chicago** study); **girls and weaker students** gained most.
[4:6:4] - **Samagra Shiksha (2018)** merged **SSA, RMSA, Teacher Education**; **PM SHRI** (Pradhan Mantri Schools for Rising India) launched **2022** as NEP 2020 models.
[4:6:5] # School mergers
[4:6:6] - Restructuring disrupted rural schooling (some children missed up to six months); **village protests over shifting Classes 3–5** to "Model Primary Schools"; example **Bangaramma Kandriga** (2.5 km walk).
[4:6:7] - **UDISE data (2014–2024)**: government schools **fell 4%**; private schools **rose 7%**. **UDISE+ = Unified District Information System for Education**.
[4:6:8] - **U-DISE+ 2024-25 (provisional):** **58,572 schools** (38,222 primary, 7,526 upper primary, 12,824 high schools; 44,292 in the State Govt sector, 13,484 private unaided); **3.13 lakh teachers**; enrolment **68.27 lakh**. Old APPSC key for 'number of schools (nearest)': **61,500**.
[4:6:9] TABLE head: Government | Model
  [4:6:9.0] YSRCP (2019–24) | School Rationalisation Policy (2023): primary schools only Classes 1–2; Classes 3–5 merged with high schools
  [4:6:9.1] NDA–TDP (current) | Model Primary Schools (one per panchayat) for Classes 3–5; others downgraded to Classes 1–2
[4:6:10] - **Both rounds NEP 2020-aligned**; model school gaps: 1 usable classroom, 3 teachers instead of 5, 1 shared washroom.
[4:6:11] - **Amma Vodi (YSRCP) → Thalliki Vandanam (TDP)**: **₹13,000/year per child** to mothers; private schools collect it.
[4:6:12] - **RTE Act, 2009** norm: school within **1 km for Classes 1–5**; mergers forced 2–3 km walks.

## SUB 8 | MEPMA, SHG Women and Youth
[4:8:0] # MEPMA basics
[4:8:1] - **MEPMA = Mission for Elimination of Poverty in Municipal Areas**: **urban** poor through SHGs, works through **ULBs** (not rural). Director / Mission Director **N. Tej Bharat**.
[4:8:2] # MEPMA initiatives
[4:8:3] TABLE head: Initiative | Key facts
  [4:8:3.0] LEAP (Livelihoods & Entrepreneurship Acceleration Plan) | launched at Tadepalli, 10 April 2025; one entrepreneur per household; base 28.7 lakh women in 2.74 lakh SHGs; target 30,000 women entrepreneurs (10,000 scale-up + 20,000 new) by 8 June 2025; partner India SME Forum
  [4:8:3.1] MLCC app (MEPMA Loan Charge Creation) | from 1 June all urban SHG loans only via MLCC; TOT at MEPMA HQ Tadepalli with SLBC and banks
  [4:8:3.2] SHG Profiling App | 2,74,873 SHGs, 28,39,046 members; 99.41% coverage; Aadhaar-linked
  [4:8:3.3] Trupti Canteens | about 3,000 SHG women to run 700 canteens; each earns about ₹30,000 a month; linked to Smart Street Programme
  [4:8:3.4] MoUs (26 August 2025) | vision "One Family, One Entrepreneur"
[4:8:4] - MEPMA loans: **₹16,000 crore** to SHGs in **nine months**; data digitisation **99%**.
[4:8:5] - Four new urban livelihood initiatives: **Rapido, Trupti Hotels, Smart Street, Green Livelihoods**; **MEPMA–Rapido**: electric two/three-wheelers to **1,000+** SHG women.
[4:8:6] - First Trupti canteen: **Atmakur Bus Stand Centre, Nellore**, inaugurated by MA&UD Minister **Dr Ponguru Narayana**, with **Sara Project**, run by **4** MEPMA women.

## SUB 9 | Cleanliness, Environment and Urban Governance
[4:9:0] # Swachha Andhra Awards–2025
[4:9:1] - Launched **1 September 2025**: **AP first State in India to institutionalise annual cleanliness and sanitation awards** (**52 State-level** awards); **GO-RT-916** of MAUD; modelled on **Swachh Survekshan**.
[4:9:2] - Categories: municipalities, gram panchayats, schools, hospitals, bus stations, Rythu Bazaars, hostels, industries, anganwadis, residential schools; NGOs and sanitation workers as **"Swachhata Warriors"**.
[4:9:3] - **First ceremony: 2 October 2025 (Gandhi Jayanti)**.
[4:9:4] - **Swarna Andhra–Swachha Andhra (SASA)**: **every third Saturday = Swachha Andhra Day**.
[4:9:5] # AQLI 2025 - air pollution
[4:9:6] - AP average PM2.5 **26 µg/m³** (about 5× WHO) → life expectancy loss **2.1 years** (lower than national **3.5 years**).
[4:9:7] - **Visakhapatnam 40–50 µg/m³** (about 8× WHO); Vijayawada 30–40 µg/m³.
[4:9:8] - Trend: **17.8 µg/m³ (1998) → above 30 µg/m³ (2021)**, rise over **70%**; 50% of AP exposed to >30 µg/m³.
[4:9:9] - India average PM2.5 (2023) **41 µg/m³**; meeting WHO limit adds 3.5 years; northern plains could gain 5 years.
[4:9:10] TABLE head: Standard | PM2.5 annual | PM10 annual
  [4:9:10.0] WHO (2021) | 5 µg/m³ (from 10) | 15 µg/m³ (from 20)
  [4:9:10.1] India NAAQS | 40 µg/m³ | 60 µg/m³
[4:9:11] - **NCAP (2019)**: 20–30% cut by 2024 (extended to **2026**); **132 non-attainment cities**.
[4:9:12] # Other governance items
[4:9:13] - **National Efficient Cooking Programme** in all Anganwadis: about **₹24 crore** saved a year; **28,327 tonnes CO₂** cut; AP delegation led by **A. Surya Kumari**; praised by WCD Secretary Anil Malik and **BEE**.
[4:9:14] - **Aspirational Blocks Programme**: **Ibrahimpatnam and Penuganchiprolu** (NTR district) won **NITI Aayog gold** for 100% results in 6 indicators; dashboard tracks **49 indicators**; ABP launched **7 January 2023**, **500 blocks**.
[4:9:15] - **Puramithra app** (Vijayawada Municipal Corporation): property and vacant land tax, grievances with photos; **5% discount** on advance tax 2025–26.
[4:9:16] - **CAG** delegation (28 May 2025) at **TUDA** (Tirupati Urban Development Authority; statutory body under the AP Urban Development Authorities Act): recommended **digitising financial and accounting systems**; reviewed land acquisition, layouts, zoning, parks.

[4:10:4] # FIRST / LAST / SPECIAL FACTS
[4:10:5] - First Swachha Andhra Awards ceremony: 2 October 2025.
[4:10:6] - Polavaram district HQ: Rampachodavaram.
[4:10:7] - Peddaharivanam: new mandal, from Adoni (Kurnool).
[4:10:8] - Only constituencies wholly in one division in old Krishna: Kaikaluru and Nuzvid.
[4:10:9] - Asia's first women's university: SNDT, Mumbai (1916).
[4:10:10] - Excise Suraksha App live from 16 October 2025; DLGVS G.O. 376 of 15 October 2025.
[4:10:11] # Traps
[4:10:12] - 1. **Insurance up to ₹2.5 lakh, Trust ₹2.5–25 lakh**, not the reverse; the ₹25 lakh is **per family**.
[4:10:13] - 2. **Margadarsi = mentor; Bangaru Kutumbam = poor family**; mentors are voluntary.
[4:10:14] - 3. **Polavaram district HQ = Rampachodavaram**; final count 28, not 29.
[4:10:15] - 4. **Auto Mitra** is a State scheme, not Union PM-SYM; **Navodayam 2.0** is a State programme.
[4:10:16] - 5. **MEPMA** is for **urban** poor, not rural.
[4:10:17] - 6. **Budget 2025–26 reduced** customs duty on fish/shrimp feed.
[4:10:18] - 7. **BPS 2025 excludes** the APCRDA Capital City Area and public land.
[4:10:19] - 8. **Peeleru** division is in the Madanapalle area, not Markapuram.
[4:10:20] - 9. **Chandranna Vidyut Velugulu** helps small industries and traders, not large industries.
[4:10:21] - 10. Traffic warnings before challans follow the **Kerala** model.

  x: Ibrahimpatnam and Penuganchiprolu blocks of NTR district received the gold medals.

  x: The Swachha Andhra Awards–2025 cover municipalities, gram panchayats, schools, hospitals and several other institutions, while NGOs and sanitation workers are recognised as “Swachhata Warriors”. The awards are therefore not restricted to only urban local bodies or educational institutions.

  x: Andhra Pradesh's average PM2.5 concentration increased from 17.8 µg/m³ in 1998 to more than 30 µg/m³ in 2021, a rise of over 70%. The other options reverse or replace the reported trend.

[ca0918c3083578] sub9
  According to the WHO 2021 update cited in the Air Quality Life Index 2025 context, what is the annual PM2.5 standard?
  15 µg/m³ | *5 µg/m³ | 40 µg/m³ | 60 µg/m³
  x: The WHO annual PM2.5 standard after the 2021 update is 5 µg/m³. The values 15 µg/m³ and 40 µg/m³ relate to other particulate-matter standards, including India's NAAQS, while 60 µg/m³ is India's annual PM10 standard.
